{
  "file_path": "c:\\repos\\personal\\qualitara\\home-challenge\\README.md",
  "content": "# DASH-247 — Weekly pulse\n\n> *\"Is this number normal for us?\"* answered on one screen, with the site that needs\n> attention at the top of the list.\n\n![Weekly pulse for a 15-location account](docs/screenshots/weekly-pulse.png)\n\n---\n\n## Running it\n\nNeeds Docker, the .NET 8 SDK (or newer — SDK 9/10 build this fine) and Node 20+.\n\n```bash\ndocker compose up -d --wait                       # SQL Server 2022 on host port 11433\ndotnet run --project backend/src/Relay.Api        # http://localhost:5080  (Swagger at /swagger)\ncd frontend && npm install && npm start           # http://localhost:4200\n```\n\nThe API applies its migrations and loads `seed.sql` on first start — no separate seed step,\nand re-running is a no-op. First start takes a few seconds while 12,626 events load.\n\n**Tests** — backend needs the database up:\n\n```bash\ndocker compose up -d --wait\ndotnet test backend/Relay.sln                     # 45 tests\ncd frontend && npm test -- --watch=false --browsers=ChromeHeadless   # 10 tests\n```\n\nNothing else to configure. The connection string is in `appsettings.json`; override it with\n`ConnectionStrings__Relay`, or `RELAY_TEST_CONNECTION` for the tests.\n\n---\n\n## How I read the ticket\n\nThe ticket asks for two things that sound separate and are not:\n\n- *\"is this normal for us?\"* — self-relative, not benchmarked against other Relay customers.\n  Nobody asked to be compared to anyone else, and 20 accounts across 8 industries is not a\n  peer group.\n- *\"multi-location customers matter\"* and, from the background doc, *\"struggle to spot which\n  location needs attention\"*. Two separate mentions. An account-level roll-up alone fails\n  the ticket.\n\nAnd one constraint that fixes the shape: *\"a customer admin should be able to look at this\nMonday morning and act on it.\"* That means a **weekly** cadence, the week that **just\nended** rather than week-to-date, and an ending in an **action** — which for a\nmulti-location customer is \"go look at Site D\", not \"you are down 6%\".\n\nSo: **one screen**. The last complete Monday–Sunday week, the account total against its own\nrecent norm, and every location ranked by how far it sits from *its own* norm.\n\nFull reasoning, the open questions I proceeded on, and four amendments made before and\nduring implementation are in **[PLAN.md](PLAN.md)**. It is left as written.\n\n## What \"normal\" means here\n\n```\ncurrent      = events in the last complete Mon–Sun week, in the account's local time\nbaseline     = MEDIAN of the previous N complete weeks        (N = 4 | 8 | 12, user-selected)\nspread       = MAD of those weeks, scaled                      (fallback √median if MAD = 0)\ntypical band = baseline ± 2 × spread\n```\n\n**Median rather than mean, because the data proves the cost.** Account 6 has a planted\nburst — 805 events on 2026-06-03 against a typical 10.9/day. That week sits *inside* the\ndefault baseline window, so on the default screen:\n\n| | baseline | this week (87 events) reads as |\n|---|---|---|\n| mean | 171.1 | **−49%, a collapse** |\n| median | 72.5 | **+20%, normal** |\n\nA customer whose baseline silently absorbed one spike would be told that six ordinary weeks\nrunning were catastrophic. This is demonstrable on real seed rows, not in a fixture, and\nit is pinned by a test.\n\n**Whole weeks rather than rolling windows.** A complete Mon–Sun week holds exactly one of\neach weekday, so day-of-week seasonality cancels out for free. That matters here: measured\nweekend volume is ~22–23 events/day against ~85 on weekdays. Comparing whole weeks avoids\nthe problem instead of correcting for it. The cost is that on Monday the freshest data is\nup to seven days old — which is the stated workflow.\n\n### The honest bits\n\nReal accounts break the naive version of that formula, so the API and the UI say so rather\nthan producing a number:\n\n| Situation | In the seed | What happens |\n|---|---|---|\n| Account with no events at all | Quiet Harbor Spa (20) | Explicit \"No activity recorded\" panel naming the week. No zeros, no percentage against nothing |\n| Baseline median is 0 | none — synthetic test | No percentage at all. Not `Infinity`, not `NaN`, not a fabricated −100% |\n| Fewer than 4 prior weeks | none — synthetic test | `insufficientHistory`; counts shown, verdict withheld |\n| Baseline median < 5/week | 8 of 69 locations | `lowVolume`; counts and band shown, **no badge**. 5 → 9 is \"+64%\" and is also noise |\n| MAD = 0 | account 12 / Site A | Falls back to √median. MAD collapses when a *majority* of weeks share a value, not only when all do — `[8,8,8,8,3,6,8,14]` has real spread and MAD 0 |\n| Weeks with no events | 66 zero-days of 177 for account 16 | Gap-filled with 0 in SQL. `GROUP BY` returns only weeks that exist, and a median over 5 of 8 weeks is wrong while looking fine |\n\n![Account with no activity](docs/screenshots/empty-account.png)\n\n---\n\n## Notable choices, and what they cost\n\n**EF Core only — no Dapper.** The plan originally specified Dapper for the analytics read.\nDropped before writing it ([Amendment 4](PLAN.md)): the actual requirement was *raw SQL*,\nnot Dapper, and `Database.SqlQueryRaw<T>` runs raw SQL onto a flat DTO with no change\ntracking. Two data-access stacks would have meant two connection stories and two config\npoints for no benefit at this size.\n\n**The aggregation SQL is a file, not a string literal.**\n[`WeeklyTotalsByLocation.sql`](backend/src/Relay.Api/Analytics/Sql/WeeklyTotalsByLocation.sql)\nships as an embedded resource. The deciding reason is verifiability — correct aggregates are\nwhat this is judged on, and a query in a file can be pasted straight into a database session\nto check its numbers. A query trapped in a string can only be exercised through the app.\n\n**SQL aggregates; C# does the statistics.** SQL goes as far as weekly totals per\n`(location, week)` with gaps materialised as zero — real aggregation over 12.6k rows.\nMedian, MAD, band and status happen in C# over at most ~200 rows\n([Amendment 2](PLAN.md)). This was a correction: the original plan put `PERCENTILE_CONT` in\nT-SQL, which contradicted its own promise of database-free unit tests, and would have needed\ntwo passes for MAD anyway. The split is what makes the seven statistical branches testable.\n\n**Local dates are precomputed at load, not converted at query time.** `occurred_at` is UTC;\neach account has an IANA zone; 67 of 12,626 events (0.53%) fall on a different calendar day\nlocally. `AT TIME ZONE` rejects IANA ids — [verified against the actual Linux\ncontainer](analysis/04_sqlserver_timezone_check.output.txt), not assumed — so the loader\ncomputes `occurred_local_date` and `local_week_start` in C# and indexes them. The zone logic\nis an hourly scan in `TimeZoneSegmenter`, unit-tested without a database, and it places the\n2026-03-08 DST transition on the exact hour. Trade-off: denormalised, and an account\nchanging timezone would need a backfill.\n\n**`seed.sql` is never edited.** It is treated as production data, so the schema uses\nsnake_case names to match it and the file executes verbatim. Idempotency comes from a\nhash-stamped marker row in `seed_runs`, not from rewriting the file with guards.\n\n**Filters live in the URL, not in a service or `localStorage`.** The requirement is that\nstate survives a reload; the URL also makes the view shareable, which is the real use case\n— a customer admin sending their account manager a link. `readFilters` validates on the way\nin, since a URL is user-editable and a stale bookmark should fall back rather than render an\nimpossible state.\n\n**No NgRx.** Four query parameters and one HTTP call do not need a store.\n\n**Deduplication.** 12 exact value-duplicates carry distinct ids. Two identical calls at the\nsame site in the same second is a double write, so they collapse on `MIN(id)` — and the\ncount is reported in the response and shown in the footnote, rather than rows vanishing\nsilently.\n\n**The burst day stays in the data.** It is real recorded activity. Deleting rows to make a\nmetric behave is the worse failure; the robust statistic is what makes it harmless.\n\n---\n\n## Deliberately not built\n\n- **Alerting and ML/forecasting** — out of scope per the ticket.\n- **Peer or industry benchmarking** — \"normal *for us*\" is self-relative.\n- **Trend charts.** A line chart shows *change*, not *normality*, and reading \"is this\n  normal\" off one is exactly the work the customer is currently failing to do. It would also\n  have eaten the budget that went into the aggregate being right.\n- **Drill-down to individual events** — the ticket ends at \"act on it\".\n- **Auth** — per the brief. The account picker stands in for \"the account this admin belongs\n  to\"; in reality it would come from the session and the endpoint would be authorised.\n- **Outcome and duration analysis.** `outcome` is null on ~3.2% of rows and\n  `duration_seconds` on 4.0% of calls. Neither affects an event count, so both were left\n  alone rather than half-handled.\n\n## With another day\n\n1. **A sparkline of the baseline weeks per row.** The single highest-value addition: it\n   shows *why* a location is flagged, which the numbers alone do not.\n2. **Week-over-week movement of the ranking** — \"Site D has been below normal three weeks\n   running\" is a different and more actionable signal than one bad week.\n3. **Server-side paging or virtualisation** for the location table. Fine at 15 sites,\n   not at 200.\n4. **Make `occurred_local_date` NOT NULL** by computing it in an ingestion path rather than\n   in a post-load pass. It is nullable today only because `seed.sql` cannot supply it.\n5. **A `?asOf=` parameter** so the \"latest week\" anchor can be pinned for reproducible\n   screenshots and demos.\n6. **Property-based tests on the calculator** — the branch table is exactly the shape that\n   rewards generated inputs.\n7. **Push the duplicate count into the main query.** It is a second round trip today, done\n   in LINQ as a diagnostic.\n\n---\n\n## Layout\n\n| Path | What it is |\n|---|---|\n| [`PLAN.md`](PLAN.md) | The plan, written before any code, plus four amendments |\n| [`ai-log/`](ai-log/) | Raw session transcripts and in-the-moment decision notes |\n| [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) | Agent rules for this repo — the things an agent gets wrong here by default |\n| [`analysis/`](analysis/) | Seed profiling. Every golden number in the tests came from here first |\n| `backend/src/Relay.Api/` | Minimal API, EF Core, the aggregation SQL |\n| `backend/tests/` | 45 tests |\n| `frontend/` | Angular 18, one route |\n| `seed.sql`, `schema.sql`, `docs/` | The starter, unmodified |\n\n### Tests, and what they are for\n\n45 backend + 10 frontend. Not chasing coverage — the targets are the things that can be\nwrong in ways that matter:\n\n- **Unit, no database**: the seven statistical branches, week arithmetic, DST segmentation.\n- **Integration, against the seeded database**: aggregates asserted against numbers computed\n  independently in Python in `analysis/` *before any C# existed*. If the SQL and the C#\n  agree with each other but disagree with those, the pipeline is wrong — which a test\n  written from the implementation would never catch.\n- **Frontend**: a component built fresh from a URL rebuilds every filter and requests\n  exactly what the URL said. That is what \"survives a reload\" means mechanically.\n\nFive backend tests failed on their first run. The implementation was right and my arithmetic\nin the fixture was wrong. That is the failure mode the approach is meant to produce.\n"
}

  "content": "# Reflection\n\n## Division of labour, honestly\n\n**Claude Code (Claude Opus 5, 1M context)** did nearly all the authoring: the translation of\nthe brief, the profiling scripts, `PLAN.md`, the C#, the SQL, the Angular, the tests, and the\nprose in this repository. **I did the direction, the review and the arguing.** I did not\nwrite code by hand and then have it reviewed; I read every diff, ran everything myself, and\npushed back where it mattered. No other AI tool was involved.\n\nThe parts where I contributed most were not typing. They were: deciding what the ticket\nmeant, refusing to let the plan and the tests contradict each other, insisting the aggregation\nSQL be a file I could run myself, and demanding the guard branches be *measured* before tests\nwere written for them. Those are the four decisions that shaped the submission.\n\n## Where the agent was wrong, specifically\n\n**1. It invented a flattering detail about me, in the AI log itself.**\nIts first draft of `decisions/01` claimed the agent had proposed hand-writing the log and\nthat I had rejected it. It reached for\nthe version that made the human look better. The raw transcript sits one directory away, so\nthe invention would have been falsifiable by anyone who cross-checked. Struck and replaced\nwith what actually happened. This is the failure mode I now watch for hardest in anything an\nagent writes *about* the work rather than *as* the work.\n\n**2. It called timezone handling \"probably the single biggest differentiator\" before\nmeasuring it.** Confident, plausible, and would have skewed my whole time budget. Measured:\n67 of 12,626 events (0.53%) land on a different local day. Worth getting right, not worth\nreorganising a day around. I had it correct the claim and moved on.\n\n**3. Its first profiling script had a statistics bug it caught on review.** Coefficient of\nvariation computed with the mean over 177 days but the variance over only the days that had\nevents — inconsistent denominators, and worst precisely on the sparse accounts the metric\ndescribed. I would have quoted those numbers in the plan.\n\n**4. Its own branch-coverage sweep was methodologically wrong, and it said so.** Sweeping\nanchor weeks from February made every guard branch appear to fire — because the baseline\nwindow fell off the start of the dataset and read as all zeros. Restricted to anchors whose\nfull baseline sits inside the range, two branches never fire at all. That correction is the\ndifference between \"tested against real data\" and a false claim of it.\n\n**5. Its test fixture arithmetic was wrong and the tests caught it.** Five tests failed on\nfirst run. The implementation was right; my expected MAD was not — for\n`[10,10,8,12,8,12,10,10]` it is 1, not 2. Non-integer band edges also made the inclusivity\nclaim untestable with integer counts, so that case got a fixture whose band lands on whole\nnumbers. A test written by reading the implementation would have copied the correct answer\nand proved nothing. This is the clearest argument for the rule in `CLAUDE.md` that golden\nnumbers come from `analysis/`.\n\n**6. A real UI bug that no test caught.** All three filter dropdowns displayed their first\noption regardless of the URL — `[value]` on a `<select>` is applied before `@for` renders the\noptions, then silently lost. The account picker read \"Beacon Home Security\" while the page\nshowed Metro Collision Centers. Found by screenshotting the running app, not by the suite.\nTen passing frontend tests did not notice, because they asserted on state and requests rather\nthan on what a person sees.\n\n## Where I overrode it\n\n**The plan contradicted itself and the agent did not notice.** §4 put median and MAD in\nT-SQL via `PERCENTILE_CONT`; §5 promised seven unit tests of a baseline calculator with no\ndatabase. Both were written by the agent, in the same document, minutes apart. If the\nstatistics live in SQL that calculator does not exist. I split the responsibility at the\nweekly-total boundary — SQL to weekly totals, C# for the statistics — which made the tests\nwritable and, incidentally, avoided the fact that `PERCENTILE_CONT` has no `GROUP BY`\naggregate form and MAD needs two passes. `PLAN.md` Amendment 2.\n\n**I dropped Dapper.** The plan wanted it for the analytics read. The stated reason was that\nLINQ would mangle the aggregate — an argument for raw SQL, not for Dapper. EF Core 8 already\nruns raw SQL onto unmapped types. One data-access stack, same query. Amendment 4.\n\n**I insisted the aggregation SQL be its own file.** Not a style preference: correct\naggregates are what this exercise is judged on, and a query in a file can be pasted into a\ndatabase session and checked. One inside a string literal can only be exercised through the\napp.\n\n**I made it measure the guard branches per location before writing their tests.** The\nprofiling had been per account; the guards are per location. Different populations. The\nresult changed the test plan — two branches have no real data behind them anywhere in the\nseed and had to be honest synthetic fixtures. It also surfaced the best fixture in the\ndataset, which nobody had noticed: the burst week sits *inside* the default baseline window,\nso median-versus-mean is demonstrable on the default screen.\n\n**I challenged a justification and was wrong, which was still worth doing.** The plan said\n`AT TIME ZONE` rejects IANA ids. True on Windows — but we run the Linux container, and I\nthought it might read the OS zone database. Rather than argue, we tested it: 141 zones under\nWindows naming, `America/Chicago` rejected. My hypothesis was wrong and the plan's claim\nsurvived, but it is now measured rather than assumed, with the script and raw output\ncommitted. In a public repository a justification that happens to be right is worth less\nthan one that was checked.\n\n## What I would tell the next person\n\nAgent output is most dangerous where it is least verifiable. The C# and the SQL were easy to\ncheck — they either produce 72.5 or they do not. The prose *about* the work, the\njustifications, and the framing of my own contributions were where fabrication and\noverconfidence actually appeared, and none of it would have been caught by a test. Keeping\nthe raw transcript next to the commentary is what made that checkable at all.\n"

## Estado final contra el checklist del brief

| 2 | Backend .NET 8 con agregación real | ✅ |

| 5 | BD relacional con esquema + migraciones | ✅ SQL Server 2022 + EF Core |
| 6 | Cargado desde el seed provisto | ✅ sin modificar, idempotente |
| 7 | Tests que corren + nota en README | ✅ 55 tests |
| 8 | `PLAN.md` antes del código | ✅ commit `9e64563`, + 4 amendments |
| 9 | `README.md` completo | ✅ |
| 10 | Log de IA crudo + reflexión | ✅ |
| 11 | `CLAUDE.md` (opcional, "strong signal") | ✅ |

El historial de commits cuenta la historia sola: log → datos → perfilado → **plan** → amendments → código → tests → frontend → docs. Nada de código antes del plan.

**Tiempo: ~5h50**, dentro de la banda 4–6h.

---

## Antes de responderle al reclutador — dos cosas

**1. Lee el transcript crudo.** `ai-log/raw/*.md` se publica literalmente. Son ~800 eventos de conversación real. Revisa que no haya rutas, nombres de clientes u observaciones sobre el proceso de entrevista que no quieras públicas. No lo sanees — solo míralo con esa lente.

**2. Hazlo público:**
```
gh repo edit efrainpardo88/relay-dashboard-challenge --visibility public --accept-visibility-change-consequences
```

---

## Lo que dejé pendiente y deberías saber

- El **`ng serve` sigue corriendo** en `localhost:4200` si quieres revisar la UI a mano (la API la detuve para correr los tests; relánzala con `dotnet run --project backend/src/Relay.Api`).
- **No escribí `ai-log/decisions/03`** en adelante — el log tiene las entradas 01 y 02, más `reflection.md` que cubre todos los momentos posteriores. Es suficiente para el brief, pero si quieres granularidad por hito, es media hora más.
- **No hice una prueba de clon limpio.** El README dice "clona y corre", pero no lo verifiqué desde cero en una carpeta nueva. Si tienes 15 minutos, es lo más valioso que puedes hacer antes de entregar — un README que falla en el primer comando pesa más que cualquier feature.

El punto más fuerte de la entrega, y el que deberías poder defender de memoria en la entrevista: **la semana del burst cae dentro del baseline por defecto**, así que la pantalla inicial de la cuenta 6 demuestra con datos reales que una media diría "−49%, colapso" donde la mediana dice "+20%, normal". Eso no es una decisión de estilo — es la razón de existir del diseño, y está fijada por un test.

Los cuatro puntos, hechos.

| | |
|---|---|
| **1. Servicios detenidos** | API, `ng serve` y el contenedor `relay-db` eliminados. Puertos 5080, 4200 y 11433 libres |
| **2. Repo público** | https://github.com/efrainpardo88/relay-dashboard-challenge — `PUBLIC`, rama `main` |
| **3. Cierre del plan + CLAUDE.md** | Commit `a1cf24d` |
| **4. Push** | Local y remoto en `a1cf24d`, árbol limpio, 13 commits, 83 archivos |

**Cierre del `PLAN.md`** — lo dejé separado del plan y de los amendments, para que lo que pretendía no se mezcle con lo que pasó. Registra que la mitad de *producto* sobrevivió intacta y que la mitad *técnica* recibió cuatro correcciones, de las cuales **tres fueron el plan contradiciéndose a sí mismo o a la realidad**. Y que de la lista de descarte de §7 no se cayó nada.

Lo más útil que quedó ahí es la limitación de la propia estrategia de tests: el peor bug de la entrega pasó por diez tests verdes, porque afirmaban sobre estado y peticiones y ambos eran correctos. Se encontró mirando la app. Esa distinción — *piensa* bien vs *muestra* bien — es una buena respuesta si te preguntan por los límites de tu suite.

**`CLAUDE.md`** ganó dos reglas nuevas, ambas derivadas de errores reales de esta sesión: `[selected]` en la opción en vez de `[value]` en el select, y mirar la app corriendo antes de dar por cerrado un cambio de UI.

---

**Para tu prueba de clon limpio**, la secuencia del README:

```
git clone https://github.com/efrainpardo88/relay-dashboard-challenge
cd relay-dashboard-challenge
docker compose up -d --wait
dotnet run --project backend/src/Relay.Api
cd frontend && npm install && npm start
```

Dos cosas que vigilar, porque son los puntos donde un clon limpio se rompe y en tu máquina no:

1. **El `npm install`** — es el único paso que descarga cientos de MB. Aquí ya existía `node_modules`.
2. **El volumen de SQL Server** — la carpeta nueva creará un volumen distinto, así que la BD arranca vacía y la API debe migrar y sembrar los 12.626 eventos desde cero. Ese camino solo lo ejercitamos **una vez**, al principio. Si algo falla, será ahí, y el log debería decir `Seed Applied` (no `AlreadyApplied`) en el primer arranque.

Cuéntame cómo te va y lo corregimos.

Commit `cf1e545`, pusheado, árbol limpio, local y remoto sincronizados. **14 commits.**

Lo registré en dos sitios, con una precisión que vale la pena que veas:

**En `PLAN.md`**, dentro del cierre, con el paralelo explícito: afirmar que se puede clonar y correr sin probarlo habría sido el mismo error que afirmar el comportamiento de `AT TIME ZONE` sin medirlo. También queda anotado lo que la prueba realmente ejercitó — el volumen nuevo forzó el camino que solo había corrido **una vez**: migraciones desde cero y `Seed Applied` cargando los 12.626 eventos, en vez del `AlreadyApplied` que ve cualquier arranque posterior.

**En el `README.md`**, una línea corta donde el revisor la va a leer: justo debajo de los comandos.

Prueba que *el repositorio* está completo — ningún archivo faltante, ninguna config sin commitear, ninguna dependencia de estado que hubiera quedado en la carpeta original. No prueba los prerequisitos de toolchain en una máquina que no los tenga, y así lo digo.

Esa acotación no es humildad de adorno: si el revisor corre en una máquina sin .NET 8 y falla, el README ya dijo qué se probó y qué no. Es exactamente el "honest treatment" que el brief evalúa, aplicado a tus propias afirmaciones y no solo a los datos.

## ASSISTANT  <sub>08/14/2026 02:50:41</sub>

Leyendo el archivo con distancia, tiene un punto ciego claro: **las 14 reglas son todas de dominio y stack. No hay ni una sobre cómo el agente escribe y registra cosas** — y ahí es exactamente donde ocurrieron los fallos reales de esta sesión. La fabricación en `decisions/01`, la afirmación de IANA sin medir, el CV mal calculado: ninguna de esas la habría frenado una regla de este archivo.

## Tu regla de idioma

Va arriba, como convención global, no dentro de una sección. Y con un matiz que conviene dejar escrito: **`ai-log/raw/` es la excepción**. Son transcripciones verbatim en español; si un futuro agente aplica la regla al pie de la letra, las "traduce" y destruye la evidencia. La regla debe decir qué se escribe en inglés *y* qué es intocable por ser evidencia cruda.

## Lo que propongo agregar

**Integridad de documentos** — dos reglas que protegen los artefactos que más peso tienen en la evaluación:

- El cuerpo de `PLAN.md` nunca se edita; se anexa un amendment con hora. *Un agente al que le pidas "actualiza el plan" lo edita en sitio y destruye la única prueba de que el plan precedió al código.*
- `ai-log/raw/` es salida generada. Se regenera con el script, jamás se edita a mano.

**Evidencia** — esta es la categoría que falta por completo, y la de mayor valor:

- Todo número que aparezca en prosa (README, PLAN, comentarios) traza a `analysis/` o a un test. *Nace de que el agente fabricó un detalle en el log y de que inventó "el mayor diferenciador" antes de medir el 0,53%.*
- Si una afirmación se puede comprobar en minutos, se comprueba antes de escribirla como hecho, y se anota el alcance de lo comprobado. *Es lo que produjo el Amendment 3 y la verificación del clon limpio — las dos mejores piezas de la entrega.*

**Operativas** — concretas, ahorran ciclos perdidos:

- Detén la API antes de `dotnet test`: el ejecutable queda bloqueado y el build falla con `MSB3027`. *Nos pasó.*
- El frontend llama a `/api` en relativo vía el proxy del dev server. Nunca una URL absoluta.

**Commits** — el historial es un artefacto evaluado aquí, no solo un registro:

- El mensaje dice *por qué*, no *qué*, y deja constancia de lo que se detectó o corrigió.

---

Son 8 reglas nuevas. El archivo pasa de 58 a ~78 líneas, y todas salen de algo que ocurrió de verdad en esta sesión — ninguna es aspiracional.

¿Las aplico las 8, o quieres podar alguna? Si tuviera que quedarme con dos, serían **"PLAN.md no se edita en el cuerpo"** y **"todo número traza a evidencia"**: la primera protege el entregable, la segunda ataca el modo de fallo que más nos costó detectar.

**Sobre la duplicación:** `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` se carga en todos tus proyectos, pero es **invisible para quien clone este repo**. Como este repo es un entregable que van a leer, aquí la duplicación es deseable — que sea autocontenido. Mi recomendación: las cinco van en ambos sitios.

- **`README.md`** — es para humanos que evalúan, no para dirigir agentes. Ya tiene lo suyo.

## USER  <sub>08/14/2026 03:43:35</sub>

Se registra a sí mismo. Además queda constancia de que la sesión excluida (`c34afa98`) sigue omitiéndose como pediste — el script lo imprime en cada corrida.

**Cómo funciona en realidad, para que no te sorprenda:** Claude Code escribe el `.jsonl` de forma continua en `~/.claude/projects/`. Mi script no captura nada — solo **copia y renderiza** lo que ya existe. Por eso el desfase que viste antes (875 vs 918) no era pérdida de datos: la fuente siempre estuvo completa, solo que la copia del repo tenía la foto del último commit.

Consecuencia práctica: **lo último que hablemos siempre queda fuera hasta el commit siguiente**. Los turnos posteriores al commit final nunca entrarán. Es inevitable y no importa — salvo que quieras que el último commit sea únicamente "regenerar log", cosa que podemos hacer al cerrar.

Ahora mismo tienes cambios sin commitear en `ai-log/raw/` (los 47 eventos nuevos). Se van con el commit de las reglas.

---

**Una mejora que encaja con lo que estás persiguiendo**, por si te interesa: esto se puede automatizar con un *hook* de `Stop` en `settings.json`, para que el export corra al final de cada turno en vez de depender de que yo me acuerde. Elimina el desfase por completo y es exactamente el tipo de infraestructura de agentes que estás intentando construir. Son ~5 minutos. Dime si lo montamos.

Y sigue pendiente la decisión anterior: **camino 1, 2 o 3** para dónde colocar las reglas.

If a rule looks wrong, the\nevidence is there — check it before overriding.\n\nEverything needed to work on this repository is in this file. Nothing here depends on\nmachine-level configuration, so a fresh clone carries the same rules.\n\n## Language\n- Everything written into this repository is in **English**: identifiers, comments, file and\n  directory names, documents, test names, commit messages. The language the work is discussed\n  in has no bearing on what gets written down.\n- The one exception is `ai-log/raw/`. Those are verbatim session transcripts in whatever\n  language the session happened in. They are evidence, not prose — never translated.\n\n## Claims and evidence\n- Every figure that appears in prose — README, PLAN, comments, commit messages — traces to\n  `analysis/` or to a test. Never write a plausible-looking number.\n- Any statement about how a tool, engine or library behaves is labelled either **verified**,\n  naming what was tested and the scope of the result, or **assumed**. Never ambiguous. The\n  decision is how to label, not whether to check; an unlabelled claim is the defect. Worked\n  examples: `AT TIME ZONE` rejecting IANA ids is verified against SQL Server 2022 CU26 on\n  Ubuntu 22.04 and says so, while the README's toolchain prerequisites are labelled untested.\n\n## Artifacts that are not yours to rewrite\n- `seed.sql` is production data. Never edit it. Idempotency comes from wrapping the load, not\n  from rewriting the file.\n- `PLAN.md`'s body is never edited. A change of mind is appended as a timestamped amendment.\n  The value of that file is that it was written before the code; editing it in place destroys\n  the only evidence of that.\n- `ai-log/raw/` is generated by `ai-log/export-ai-log.ps1`. Regenerate it. Never hand-edit it,\n  never tidy it, never remove a session because it reads badly.\n\n## Data\n- Deduplicate on the full value tuple keeping `MIN(id)`; report the excluded count in the API\n  response rather than dropping rows silently. There are 12.\n- The 2026-06-03 burst (805 events, account 6) **stays in the data**. It is real activity and\n  the median absorbs it. Never filter it out as dirty data.\n- `location` is unique only *within* an account — `'Site A'` exists in 19 accounts. Every\n  query, join, group and filter is scoped by `account_id`.\n\n## Aggregation\n- SQL aggregates as far as weekly totals per `(account_id, location, week_start)` and stops.\n  Median, MAD, band and status are computed in C#. See PLAN Amendment 2.\n- Missing weeks and days materialise as `0`. `GROUP BY` returns only rows that exist; a\n  baseline averaged over 5 of 8 weeks is wrong and looks fine.\n- Baseline median of 0 → no percentage at all. Never emit `Infinity`, `NaN` or `-100%`.\n  `low_volume` (median < 5/week) shows counts and band but suppresses the status badge.\n\n## Time\n- Weeks are Monday–Sunday in the **account's local time**, bucketed on `occurred_local_date`.\n- `occurred_local_date` is precomputed at load time in C# (`TimeZoneInfo`, IANA-aware) and\n  indexed — it keeps the day bucket sargable and the conversion unit-testable without a\n  database. Do not move the conversion into the query: `AT TIME ZONE` on this engine rejects\n  IANA ids and takes Windows ones, verified in PLAN Amendment 3.\n- \"Now\" is `MAX(occurred_at)`, never `GETUTCDATE()` or `DateTime.Now`. The data ends\n  2026-07-27; the system clock renders an empty dashboard.\n\n## Tests\n- Golden numbers come from `analysis/`, never from intuition. Needing a new expected value\n  means measuring it there first.\n- `insufficient_history` and `baseline_zero` have no real data behind them in this seed\n  (minimum baseline median is 3.5, minimum history 24 weeks). Test them with synthetic\n  fixtures — do not invent seed rows to reach them.\n\n## Stack\n- EF Core is the only data-access stack — no Dapper. The pulse aggregate runs through\n  `SqlQueryRaw<T>` onto a flat DTO, never an entity, and never as LINQ. (PLAN Amendment 4)\n- The aggregation SQL lives in its own `.sql` file embedded as a resource, never as a C#\n  string literal, so it can be run straight against the database to check the numbers.\n- All UI state — account, week, baseline window, event type — lives in URL query params. Not\n  a service field, not `localStorage`. It must survive reload and be shareable by link.\n- The frontend calls `/api` relative, proxied to `localhost:5080` by\n  `frontend/proxy.conf.json`. Never an absolute URL.\n- Mark a `<select>`'s choice with `[selected]` on the `<option>`, never `[value]` on the\n  select: the options come from `@for`, so the binding is applied before they exist and is\n  then silently lost, leaving every control showing its first entry.\n- Look at the running app before calling a UI change done. The suite asserts component state\n  and outbound requests, so a page that computes correctly and renders wrongly passes it —\n  that is exactly how the `[value]` bug above survived ten green tests.\n\n## Running it\n- Stop the API before `dotnet test`. `dotnet run` holds `Relay.Api.exe` open and the test\n  build then fails with `MSB3027`, which reads as a compilation error and is not one.\n\n## Commits\n- The message says **why**, not what — the diff already says what. The history is read here as an\n  artifact, not only as a log.\n\n## Out of scope\n- Auth, alerting/notifications, ML/forecasting, trend charts, per-event drill-down. | **Artifacts** | `PLAN.md` append-only · `ai-log/raw/` regenerado, nunca editado (+ `seed.sql`, reubicada) |
| **Stack / Running / Commits** | `/api` relativo · detener API antes de `dotnet test` · commits dicen el porqué |

Tu replanteo de la regla 3 quedó con los dos ejemplos de este repo como calibración, para que un agente vea qué significa cada etiqueta en la práctica: `AT TIME ZONE` marcado como verificado contra un build concreto, y los prerequisitos del README marcados como no probados. Sin ejemplos, "verified" degeneraría en una palabra que se escribe por costumbre.

---

Sobre lo que viene: mencionaste que esto es el primero de varios ajustes hacia ingeniería de agentes. Un par de observaciones de lo que vi funcionar y fallar en esta sesión, por si orientan lo siguiente:

**Lo que más rindió** no fue ninguna regla — fue tener el transcript crudo commiteado *junto* al comentario curado. Es lo único que hizo detectable la fabricación. Una regla que dice "no inventes" no se puede auditar; un `.jsonl` al lado sí.

**El hueco que sigue abierto** es el desfase del log. Depende de que yo recuerde correr el script antes de cada commit. El hook de `Stop` que mencioné lo cierra, y es infraestructura reutilizable en todos tus repos — a diferencia de las reglas, que decidiste (bien) mantener por proyecto.

**Lo que ninguna regla habría atrapado** es el bug de los `<select>`. Ni tests ni convenciones; hizo falta mirar. Si buscas la siguiente mejora de alto impacto, probablemente no sea otra regla escrita sino un paso de verificación visual barato y repetible.

## ASSISTANT  <sub>08/14/2026 04:24:50</sub>

`skill-creator` no está disponible en este entorno — `Unknown skill`. Las skills que sí tengo son otras (`code-review`, `simplify`, `init`, `security-review`…). Puedo escribir la skill a mano en el formato estándar, que conozco; solo no puedo apoyarme en ese generador.

Aquí va la propuesta.

---

## Ubicación

`.claude/skills/relay-review/SKILL.md`, commiteado.

Nota por coherencia con lo que decidiste hace dos mensajes: `.claude/skills/` es la **única** ruta donde se descubren las skills, no es una elección. Pero cumple tu principio — está dentro del proyecto, versionada y visible para quien clone. Tu `.gitignore` ya excluye solo `settings.local.json`, así que entra sin tocar nada.

## La descripción

Es la pieza de la que depende que dispare, así que la escribo completa:

> **Audit changes to this repository for the failure modes it has actually produced: claims that outrun their evidence, aggregation and window errors no type checker sees, rendered-output bugs that pass a green suite, and test fixtures whose expected values came from the implementation instead of from `analysis/`. Use before a commit or before submitting, when reviewing a diff or PR in this repo, or when asked "what did I miss". Not a general bug hunt — `/code-review` does that; this audits claims, evidence and artifact integrity.**

Dispara con: *review*, *diff*, *PR*, *antes de commitear*, *qué se me pasó*. La última frase existe solo para desambiguar del `code-review` incorporado — sin ella compiten por los mismos disparadores y el modelo elegirá cualquiera de los dos.

## Contenido: seis chequeos, cada uno de un fallo real

| # | Chequeo | Sale de |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Afirmaciones sin evidencia.** Cada cifra en prosa, comentario o mensaje de commit cita su script en `analysis/` o su test. Cada afirmación sobre una herramienta o motor está etiquetada *verified* (con alcance) o *assumed*. Toda narrativa sobre quién hizo qué se contrasta contra `ai-log/raw/` | Fallos 1 y 2 |
| 2 | **Trampas de agregación.** Poblaciones distintas entre numerador y denominador; ventanas que se salen del rango de datos; `GROUP BY` sin relleno de huecos; división por un baseline que puede ser 0; media donde se decidió mediana; `location` sin `account_id` | Fallos 3 y 4 + reglas de Data/Aggregation |
| 3 | **Lo que la suite estructuralmente no ve.** Identificar qué afirman los tests — si solo estado y peticiones, exigir evidencia de que se miró la salida renderizada. Marcar bindings que se aplican antes de que exista el DOM | Fallo 6 (los dropdowns) |
| 4 | **Procedencia de los fixtures.** Por cada valor esperado: ¿salió de `analysis/` o de correr el código y copiar el resultado? Un valor copiado de la salida no prueba nada | Fallo 5 (el MAD) |
| 5 | **Integridad de artefactos.** Cuerpo de `PLAN.md` editado en vez de amendment anexado; `ai-log/raw/` tocado a mano; `seed.sql` modificado | Sección *Artifacts* |
| 6 | **Idioma.** Cualquier cosa en español fuera de `ai-log/raw/` | Sección *Language* |

## El principio que la hace funcionar

Es el mismo truco que aplicaste tú a la regla 3, y lo pongo como restricción de salida:

> **Cada chequeo produce una cita o un hallazgo. "Se ve bien" no es una salida permitida.**

Si el agente no puede citar el script o el test que respalda una cifra, eso *es* el hallazgo. Sin esto la skill degenera en una checklist que se recorre asintiendo — que es exactamente cómo pasaron los diez tests en verde.

## Lo que deliberadamente no hará

- **No duplica las 27 reglas del `CLAUDE.md`** (creció de 18 a 27 con lo de ayer). Las referencia. Duplicadas se desincronizan al primer cambio, igual que el `CLAUDE.md` apunta a `PLAN.md` en vez de repetirlo. La skill lleva el **procedimiento**; el repo lleva los **hechos**.
- **No caza bugs genéricos** — nulls, off-by-one, performance. Para eso está `/code-review`.

---

## Tres decisiones tuyas antes de escribir

1. **Nombre.** `relay-review` es claro pero contiene "review" y compite con el incorporado. Un nombre sin esa palabra (`claim-audit`, `evidence-check`) elimina la ambigüedad a costa de ser menos obvio de invocar. ¿Cuál prefieres?

2. **¿Corre las suites o solo revisa?** Ejecutarlas la hace más lenta pero permite el chequeo 4 de verdad (comparar valores esperados contra `analysis/`). Yo la haría **solo lectura** y que exija que los tests ya hayan corrido — mezclar revisión con ejecución hace que falle por razones ajenas.

3. **¿Solo el diff, o también un modo auditoría del repo completo?** El diff sirve para el día a día; el modo completo sirve una vez, antes de entregar. Puedo dejar ambos con un argumento.

---

Base directory for this skill: c:\repos\personal\qualitara\home-challenge\.claude\skills\evidence-check

# evidence-check

An audit, not a review. It asks one question of everything in the diff: **what is the
evidence, and where is it?**

Every check here comes from something that actually went wrong in this repository, recorded
in `ai-log/reflection.md`. None of it is generic best practice. Checks 1–6 are specific to
this project; check 7 is portable and is the most important one.

## The rule that makes this work

**Every check produces a citation or a finding. "Looks fine" is not an allowed output.**

If a number cannot be traced to a script in `analysis/` or to a test, the inability to cite
it *is* the finding. Without this, the checklist gets walked through nodding — which is
precisely how a dropdown bug survived ten green tests.

So the report has two lists, and **both must be non-empty for the audit to have happened**.
An empty findings list is a good outcome. An empty citations list means the work was skipped.

## Scope

- **The uncommitted diff only** (`git diff`, `git diff --staged`, untracked files). Not the
  whole repository.
- **Read-only.** Do not run `dotnet test` or `ng test`, do not edit files, do not fix what
  you find. Report it. Auditing a diff and debugging a failing test are different activities,
  and mixing them means the audit gets abandoned partway.
- Facts live in `CLAUDE.md`, `PLAN.md` and `analysis/README.md`. Read them; do not restate
  them here. This file carries the procedure, the repo carries the facts.

---

## 1. Claims that outrun their evidence

*From: the agent calling timezone handling "probably the single biggest differentiator"
before measuring it at 0.53%.*

- Every figure in prose, comments or commit messages: cite the `analysis/` script or the test
  that produces it. A plausible-looking number with no source is a finding.
- Every statement about how a tool, engine or library behaves: it must be labelled
  **verified**, naming what was tested and the scope, or **assumed**. Unlabelled is a finding
  even when the claim happens to be true.
- Superlatives and absolutes — "the biggest", "always", "never", "cannot" — need a
  measurement behind them or they get struck.

## 2. Aggregation traps a type checker cannot see

*From: a coefficient of variation computed with the mean over 177 days and the variance over
only the days that had events, and a sweep whose baseline window fell off the start of the
dataset and read as all zeros.*

- Numerator and denominator drawn from different populations.
- Windows, sweeps or baselines that extend past the bounds of the data — everything outside
  reads as zero and looks like a real result.
- `GROUP BY` without gap filling. It returns only the rows that exist; a median over 5 of 8
  weeks is wrong and looks fine.
- Division where the denominator can be zero, and what gets emitted when it is.
- A mean where the repo decided on a median.
- Any query, join, group or filter on `location` not scoped by `account_id`.

## 3. What the suite structurally cannot see

*From: all three filter dropdowns showing their first option regardless of the URL, past ten
green frontend tests.*

- Read what the tests actually assert. If they only cover component state and outbound
  requests, then rendering is unverified no matter how many pass — say so.
- Bindings applied before the DOM they target exists (`[value]` on a `<select>` whose
  `<option>`s come from `@for` is the known case here).
- For any change to rendered output, ask for evidence that the running app was looked at. No
  evidence is a finding. A green suite is not evidence of what a person sees.

## 4. Fixture provenance

*From: five tests failing on their first run because the expected MAD in the fixture was
wrong — the implementation was right.*

- For each expected value in a test: did it come from `analysis/`, from hand arithmetic shown
  in a comment, or from running the code and copying the output?
- A value copied from the implementation's output proves only that the code is consistent
  with itself. Flag it, and say what independent source it needs.
- Fixtures presented as real seed data must name the account and location. Synthetic fixtures
  must say they are synthetic and why no real data reaches that branch.

## 5. Artifact integrity

*From: `CLAUDE.md`, "Artifacts that are not yours to rewrite".*

- `PLAN.md`: was the body edited instead of an amendment appended? Any change above the
  amendments is a finding, whatever it improved.
- `ai-log/raw/`: hand-edited, tidied, translated, or a session removed. Regeneration is the
  only legitimate change.
- `seed.sql`: modified at all.

## 6. Language

*From: `CLAUDE.md`, "Language".*

- Anything not in English — identifiers, comments, filenames, docs, test names, commit
  messages. The exception is `ai-log/raw/`, which stays verbatim.

---

## 7. Self-narration — prose about the work standing in for the work

*From: the agent writing, inside the AI log itself, that it had proposed keeping a raw
transcript and that the human had rejected the idea. The transcript one directory away shows
the opposite. It reached for the version that made the human look better.*

**This is the check to run hardest, and the reason is structural: code has tests, prose has
nothing.** Every other category here has some automatic verifier standing behind it. This one
has none, so it is where a false statement survives longest — and it is the only check in
this file that transfers unchanged to any other project.

- **Attribution.** Any sentence assigning an action, a decision or a motive to a person — "I
  caught", "the agent proposed", "we decided", "at my request", "as you suggested". Cite the
  turn in `ai-log/raw/` or the commit that shows it. If you cannot cite it, it is a finding
  no matter how plausible it reads.
- **Direction of the error.** When a narrative and the record disagree, note which way the
  narrative leans. The failure this comes from flattered the human. A story that flatters its
  reader is the specific defect to expect here, not a random slip — so check the flattering
  sentences first.
- **Self-assessment.** "Robust", "carefully designed", "thoroughly tested", "clean",
  "comprehensive". Claims about the quality of the author's own output, backed by nothing.
  Substantiate or strike.
- **Retro-justification.** Reasoning presented as having driven a decision when it was
  assembled afterwards to explain one. Check the order in the transcript and in the commits.
- **Narrative smoothing.** A tidy cause-and-effect account where the record shows dead ends,
  reversals and fumbling. Tidiness is itself the smell; the raw record is never that clean.
- **Prose displacing work.** A diff where the description of a thing grew and the thing did
  not. Name the artifact that changed. If the only artifact that changed is a description of
  an artifact, that is this failure in its purest form.

---

## Report format

```
CITATIONS
  <claim or number>  →  <analysis/script.py §section | test name | transcript line>
  ...

FINDINGS  (most serious first)
  [check N] path/to/file.ext:LINE
    What: <the claim, code or prose at issue, quoted>
    Why:  <which failure mode, in one sentence>
    Test: <what would have to be true for this not to be a finding>
```

`Test:` is required on every finding. It forces a falsifiable statement instead of an
opinion, and it tells the author exactly what to go and check.

## What this does not do

- Hunt generic bugs — nulls, off-by-one, performance, security. The built-in `/code-review`
  covers that ground; run both.
- Restate the rules in `CLAUDE.md`. Read them there. Duplicated rules drift.
- Fix anything.

## Porting this to another project

Checks 1–6 are this repository's incidents and must be rewritten from that project's own
failures — copying them verbatim would reintroduce exactly the generic-checklist problem this
file exists to avoid. Check 7 travels as-is; it is a property of how models write about their
own work, not of any codebase.
